\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\end{split}\@add@centering

Figure 9.1
:
The universal ring and universal invariant as projective limits

In order to have a well-defined limit, we should prove that:

(9.8)

l^{J}_{\mathcal{N}}\left(\hat{\Gamma}^{\mathcal{N}+1,J}(K)\right)=\hat{\Gamma}^{\mathcal{N},J}(K).

Remark 9.4

Following the definition of the projection maps for the coefficient rings from relation (
8.15
), we have the property:

(9.9)

\text{pr}_{\mathcal{N}}^{J}\left(\hat{J}^{\mathcal{N}+1}(K)\right)=\hat{J}^{\mathcal{N}}(K).

Lemma 9.5

The knot invariant at level
\mathcal{N}
recovers all coloured Jones invariants with levels smaller than
\mathcal{N}
, which means that we have the following relation:

(9.10)

\bar{\bar{\psi}}^{J}_{\mathcal{N}}\left(\hat{\Gamma}^{\mathcal{N},J}(K)\right)=\hat{J}^{\mathcal{N}}(K).

Proof.

This property will follow from Theorem
2.9
together with the definition of the quotient maps, as we will see below. We notice that it is enough to prove that this relation holds when composed with the set of projections
p^{\mathcal{N},J}_{\mathcal{M}}
for all
\mathcal{M}\leqslant\mathcal{N}
.
So, we want to show:

(9.11)

p^{\mathcal{N},J}_{\mathcal{M}}\circ\bar{\bar{\psi}}_{\mathcal{N}}\left(\hat{\Gamma}^{\mathcal{N},J}(K)\right)=p^{\mathcal{N},J}_{\mathcal{M}}\circ\hat{J}^{\mathcal{N}}(K)\left(=J_{\mathcal{M}}(K)\right),\ \ \ \forall\mathcal{M}\leqslant\mathcal{N}.

By construction we have that:

(9.12)

\hat{\Gamma}^{\mathcal{N},J}(K)=g^{J}_{\mathcal{N}}\left(\Gamma^{\mathcal{N}}(\beta_{n})\right).

This means that we want to show:

(9.13)

p^{\mathcal{N},J}_{\mathcal{M}}\circ\bar{\bar{\psi}}^{J}_{\mathcal{N}}\circ g^{J}_{\mathcal{N}}\left(\Gamma^{\mathcal{N}}(\beta_{n})\right)=J_{\mathcal{N}}(K),\ \ \ \forall\mathcal{M}\leqslant\mathcal{N}.

On the other hand, we have:

\bar{\bar{\psi}}^{J}_{\mathcal{N}}\circ g^{J}_{\mathcal{N}}=\tilde{\psi}^{J}_{\mathcal{N}}
and
p^{\mathcal{N},J}_{\mathcal{M}}\circ\tilde{\psi}^{J}_{\mathcal{N}}=\psi^{J}_{\mathcal{M}}
. This means that relation (
9.13
) is equivalent to:

(9.14)

\psi^{J}_{\mathcal{M}}\left(\Gamma^{\mathcal{N}}(\beta_{n})\right)=J_{\mathcal{N}}(K),\ \ \ \forall\mathcal{M}\leqslant\mathcal{N}.

This is precisely the statement of the unification result up to level
\mathcal{N}
from the Unification Theorem
2.9
, which concludes the proof of this Lemma.
∎

Lemma 9.6

(Well behaviour of the intersection pairings when changing the level)

When changing the level from
\mathcal{N}
to
\mathcal{N}+1
, the intersection pairings recover one another through the induced map
l_{\mathcal{N}}
as below:

(9.15)

l^{J}_{\mathcal{N}}\left(\hat{\Gamma}^{\mathcal{N}+1,J}(K)\right)=\hat{\Gamma}^{\mathcal{N},J}(K).

Proof.

From the construction of our quotients, which use the kernels of specialisation maps for all levels bounded by a fixed colour, we have that the maps
\bar{\bar{\psi}}^{J}_{\mathcal{N}}
and
\bar{\bar{\psi}}^{J}_{\mathcal{N}+1}
are injective. Following Lemma
9.5
, we have the property that:

\bar{\bar{\psi}}^{J}_{\mathcal{N}}\left(\hat{\Gamma}^{\mathcal{N},J}(K)\right)=\hat{J}^{\mathcal{N}}(K).

This means that our statement is equivalent to:

\text{pr}_{\mathcal{N}}^{J}\left(\hat{J}^{\mathcal{N}+1}(K)\right)=\hat{J}^{\mathcal{N}}(K).

This is precisely the property from Remark
9.4
, and so the statement holds.
∎

As a conclusion after this discussion, we have that:

(9.16)

l^{J}_{\mathcal{N}}\left(\hat{\Gamma}^{\mathcal{N}+1,J}(K)\right)=\hat{\Gamma}^{\mathcal{N},J}(K).

This shows that there exists a well-defined element in the projective limit, which is a knot invariant:

{\large\hat{{\Huge{\Gamma}}}^{J}}(K)\in\hat{\mathbb{L}}^{J}.

The last part that we need to prove is that this universal invariant recoveres all coloured Jones invariants, for any level, as stated in relation (
2.14
):

(9.17)

J_{\mathcal{N}}(K)={\large\hat{{\Huge{\Gamma}}}^{J}}(K)|_{\psi^{u,J}_{\mathcal{N}}}.

Lemma 9.7

(Commutation of the squares from the diagram)

All squares associated to consecutive levels
\mathcal{N}
and
\mathcal{N}+1
commute.

Proof.

This comes from the commutativity when we project onto each factor and by the definition of the quotient maps.
∎

This shows that there exists a well-defined ring homomorphism between the limits, which we denote as below:

(9.18)

\hat{\psi}^{J}:\hat{\mathbb{L}}^{J}\rightarrow\hat{\Lambda}^{J}.

Following the commutativity of the squares at all levels, we have that:

(9.19)

\hat{\psi}^{J}\left({\large\hat{{\Huge{\Gamma}}}^{J}}(K)\right)=\hat{J}(K).

Also, using the commutativity of the diagrams together with Definition
8.9
we obtain that:

(9.20)

\hat{p}_{\mathcal{N}}^{J}\circ\hat{\psi}^{J}=p^{\mathcal{N},J}_{\mathcal{N}}\circ\bar{\psi}^{\mathcal{N},J}_{\mathcal{N}}=\psi^{u,J}_{\mathcal{N}}.

Now, following relation (
9.5
), we have that:

(9.21)

\hat{p}_{\mathcal{N}}^{J}(\hat{J}(K))=J_{\mathcal{N}}(K).

Using the previous three relations, we conclude that:

(9.22)

\displaystyle{\large\hat{{\Huge{\Gamma}}}^{J}}(K)|_{\psi^{u}_{\mathcal{N}}}

\displaystyle=\psi^{u,J}_{\mathcal{N}}\left({\large\hat{{\Huge{\Gamma}}}^{J}}(K)\right)=

\displaystyle=\hat{p}_{\mathcal{N}}^{J}\circ\hat{\psi}^{J}\left({\large\hat{{\Huge{\Gamma}}}^{J}}(K)\right)=

\displaystyle=\hat{p}_{\mathcal{N}}^{J}(\hat{J}(K))=

\displaystyle=J_{\mathcal{N}}(K),\ \ \ \ \forall\mathcal{N}\in\mathbb{N},\mathcal{N}\geqslant 2.

This concludes our construction and shows that we have a universal coloured Jones invariant for links
{\large\hat{{\Huge{\Gamma}}}^{J}}(K)
, constructed from graded intersections in configuration spaces, which recovers the coloured Jones invariants at all levels, through specialisation of coefficients.
∎

10
Relation between our universal invariant and Habiro-Willetts invariant

So, we have the universal invariant
{\large\hat{{\Huge{\Gamma}}}^{J}}(K)\in\hat{\mathbb{L}}^{J}
in the universal ring which is the projective limit of quotients of

\mathbb{L}=\mathbb{Z}[x^{\pm 1},d^{\pm 1}].

Proposition 10.1

(Structure of the quotient ideals)

The sequence of nested ideals in
\mathbb{L}
that we used for the universal invariant have the following form:

(10.1)

\displaystyle\tilde{I}_{\mathcal{N}}^{J}:=\langle\prod_{i=1}^{\mathcal{N}}(xd^{i-1}-1)\rangle\subseteq\mathbb{L}.

This comes from the definition of the specialisation maps from the relations (
3.17
) and (
8.6
).
Next, we know that they lead to sequences of associated quotient rings and projective limits:

(10.2)

\displaystyle\phantom{A}\hskip-227.62204pt\ l^{J}_{\mathcal{N}}\hskip 28.45274ptl^{J}_{\mathcal{N}+1}

\displaystyle\cdots\hat{\mathbb{L}}^{J}_{\mathcal{N}}\leftarrow\hat{\mathbb{L}}^{J}_{\mathcal{N}+1}\leftarrow\cdots\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \hat{\mathbb{L}}^{J}:=\underset{\longleftarrow}{\mathrm{lim}}\ \hat{\mathbb{L}}^{J}_{\mathcal{N}}

Now we will explore the correlation to the work of Habiro and Willetts. In
[
31
]
[Definition 14, Lemma 15], Willetts considered the sequence of ideals and quotients:

(10.3)

\displaystyle\tilde{I}_{\mathcal{N}}^{W}:=\langle\prod_{i=1}^{k-1}(xd^{i-1}-1)\prod_{i=k-1}^{\mathcal{N}-1}(d^{i}-1)\mid 1\leqslant k\leqslant\mathcal{N}-1\rangle\subset\mathbb{L}

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbb{L}}^{W}_{\mathcal{N}+1}=\mathbb{L}/\tilde{I}_{\mathcal{N}}^{W}

They lead to the following projective limit:

(10.4)

\displaystyle\cdots\hat{\mathbb{L}}^{W}_{\mathcal{N}}\leftarrow\hat{\mathbb{L}}^{W}_{\mathcal{N}+1}\leftarrow\cdots\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \hat{\mathbb{L}}^{W}:=\underset{\longleftarrow}{\mathrm{lim}}\ \hat{\mathbb{L}}^{W}_{\mathcal{N}}.

Remark 10.2

(Semi-simple versus non semi-simple)

For the case of the topological model for the coloured Jones polynomials, we would like to emphasise that the variable
u
does not plany any important role, as it gets anyway specialised in the same way as
x
, so we can work without it.

However, for the topological model for coloured Alexander case, the variable
u
is important and it plays a central role in each specialisation at each level
\mathcal{N}
, so we cannot separate it directly as for the case of coloured Jones universal invariants, where the representation theory is semi-simple.

Definition 10.3

(Ideals and limit rings)

As a summary, we have two sequences of ideals that lead to two universal rings, as below:

(10.5)

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbb{L}}^{J}=\underset{\longleftarrow}{\mathrm{lim}}\ \hat{\mathbb{L}}^{J}_{\mathcal{N}}=\underset{\longleftarrow}{\mathrm{lim}}\ \mathbb{L}/\tilde{I}_{\mathcal{N}}^{J}

\displaystyle\tilde{I}_{\mathcal{N}}^{J}:=\langle\prod_{i=1}^{\mathcal{N}}(xd^{i-1}-1)\rangle\subset\mathbb{L}.

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbb{L}}^{W}=\underset{\longleftarrow}{\mathrm{lim}}\ \mathbb{L}/\tilde{I}_{\mathcal{N}}^{W}

\displaystyle\tilde{I}_{\mathcal{N}}^{W}:=\langle\prod_{i=1}^{k-1}(xd^{i-1}-1)\prod_{i=k-1}^{\mathcal{N}-1}(d^{i}-1)\mid 1\leqslant k\leqslant\mathcal{N}-1\rangle\subset\mathbb{L}.

In
[
17
]
, Habiro defined a two variable power series constructed in his Universal Ring for knots, that unifies all coloured Jones polynomials and conjectured that at roots of unity this recovers coloured Alexander polynomials over the Alexander polynomials. Then Willetts changed the description of the ring introduced by Habiro, and defined the Universal invariant in this new ring, which he defined as
\hat{\mathbb{L}}^{W}
.

Theorem 10.4

(Habiro-Willetts’s Universal invariant
[
31
]
)

There exists a well-defined universal invariant in the limit ring
{\large\hat{{\Huge{\Gamma}}}^{W}}(K)\in\hat{\mathbb{L}}^{W}
such that:

(10.6)

\displaystyle\large{\displaystyle\hat{{\Huge{\Gamma}}}^{W}}(K)\Bigm|_{d=\xi_{\mathcal{N}}^{-1}}=~\frac{\Phi^{\mathcal{N}}(K,x)}{\Delta(K,x^{2\mathcal{N}})}

\displaystyle\large{\displaystyle\hat{{\Huge{\Gamma}}}^{W}}(K)\Bigm|_{x=d^{\mathcal{N}-1}}=~J_{\mathcal{N}}(K).

Theorem 10.5

(Projection onto Habiro-Willetts’s Universal invariant)

There is a natural map between the universal rings
\hat{\mathbb{L}}^{J}
and
\hat{\mathbb{L}}^{W}
which sends our universal Jones invariant to Habiro-Willetts’s universal invariant:

\pi:\hat{\mathbb{L}}^{J}\rightarrow\ \hat{\mathbb{L}}^{W}

(10.7)

\pi({\large\hat{{\Huge{\Gamma}}}^{J}}(K))={\large\hat{{\Huge{\Gamma}}}^{W}}(K).

In order to prove this Theorem, we start with a discussion concerning relations between the three type of rings and quotients that we have so far.

Remark 10.6

(Quotients and specialisation maps)

1) Let
R
be a ring and
A,B\subseteq R
. There exists a well-defined quotient map
R/A\rightarrow R/B
if and only if
A\subseteq B
.

2) Let
R,R^{\prime}
be two rings,
A\subseteq R
ideal and
f:R\rightarrow R^{\prime}
a ring homomorphism. Then there exists a well defined induced map
\bar{f}:R/A\rightarrow R^{\prime}
if and only if
A\subseteq Ker(f)
.

Remark 10.7

(Specialisation maps from the level
\mathcal{N}
quotients)

We have in mind specialisations that are induced from the quotients:

\hat{\mathbb{L}}^{J}_{\mathcal{N}},\hat{\mathbb{L}}^{W}_{\mathcal{N}}.

We have three types of ideals
\tilde{I}_{\mathcal{N}}^{J},\tilde{I}_{\mathcal{N}}^{W}
and the ones associated to the ADO invariants. We notice that:

- the ideal generated by
(xd^{\mathcal{M}-1}-1)
describes specialisations related to coloured Jones polynomials at levels
\mathcal{M}\leqslant\mathcal{N}
[
Natural parameters
]

- the ideal generated by
(d^{\mathcal{M}}-1)
describes specialisations related to coloured Alexander polynomials at levels
\mathcal{M}\leqslant\mathcal{N}
[
Roots of unity
].

Proposition 10.8

(No specialisations from Willetts’s rings to rings for coloured Jones polynomials)

Since the ideals
\tilde{I}_{\mathcal{N}}^{W}
are larger than the kernel of the above specialisations, this means that there is no well defined specialisation map from Willetts’s intermadiary rings to either generic parameters or roots of unity:

\nexists\ \psi_{A}^{W}:\hat{\mathbb{L}}^{W}_{\mathcal{N}}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}(\xi_{\mathcal{N}}^{\pm 1})

\nexists\ \psi^{W}:\hat{\mathbb{L}}^{W}_{\mathcal{N}}\rightarrow\Lambda^{J}_{\mathcal{N}}.

This comes directly from Remark
10.6
.

Remark 10.9

(No unification at intermediary levels)

So the question of having a knot invariant at Willetts’s intermediary levels that sees either coloured Jones invariants or coloured Alexander invariants is impossible to even state.

Proposition 10.10

(Relation between the two quotients and associated universal rings)

We have
\tilde{I}_{\mathcal{N}}^{J}\subseteq\tilde{I}_{\mathcal{N}}^{W}\subseteq\mathbb{Z}[x^{\pm 1},d^{\pm 1}]
. This means that at the level of quotient rings we have:

(10.8)

\displaystyle\pi_{\mathcal{N}}

\displaystyle\mathbb{Z}[x^{\pm 1},d^{\pm 1}]\rightarrow\ \hat{\mathbb{L}}^{J}_{\mathcal{N}}\xrightarrow{\ }\mathrel{\mkern-14.0mu}\rightarrow\hat{\mathbb{L}}^{W}_{\mathcal{N}}

and at the limit

(10.9)

\displaystyle\pi

\displaystyle\mathbb{Z}[x^{\pm 1},d^{\pm 1}]\rightarrow\ \hat{\mathbb{L}}^{J}\rightarrow\ \hat{\mathbb{L}}^{W}

Proof.

The relation presented in (
10.8
) comes from the fact that

\tilde{I}^{J}_{\mathcal{N}}\subseteq\tilde{I}^{W}_{\mathcal{N}}.

Then, the map
\pi:\hat{\mathbb{L}}^{J}\rightarrow\ \hat{\mathbb{L}}^{W}

at the limit comes from the property that we have an induced map between the limit rings.
∎

Now we put these elements together and show the proof of Theorem
10.5
as follows.

Proof.

Our universal invariant is a limit of the invariants
\hat{\mathbb{L}}^{J}_{\mathcal{N}}
:

\hat{\mathbb{L}}^{J}:=\underset{\longleftarrow}{\mathrm{lim}}\ \hat{\mathbb{L}}^{J}_{\mathcal{N}}.

Here, we go back and we use the connection to representation theory. We use the representation theory of
U_{q}(sl(2))
over
\mathbb{Z}[x^{\pm 1},d^{\pm 1}]
. There, there is a Verma module of infinite dimension
\hat{V}
, generated by an infinite family of vectors
\{v_{0},v_{1},...\}
. Then, for two fixed parameters
n,m
there is the associated weight space:

(see
[
2
]
[Section 2.2, Section 2.3])

\hat{V}_{n,m}=\langle v_{e_{1}}\otimes...\otimes v_{e_{n}}|e_{1}+...+e_{n}=m\rangle.

Further, if we fix a level
\mathcal{N}
, the
\mathcal{N}^{th}
finite weight space over two parameters is given by:

V^{\mathcal{N}}_{n,m}\subseteq\hat{V}_{n,m}

V^{\mathcal{N}}_{n,m}=\langle v_{e_{1}}\otimes...\otimes v_{e_{n}}|e_{1}+...+e_{n}=m,0\leqslant e_{1},...,e_{n}\leqslant\mathcal{N}\rangle.

Since
\hat{\mathbb{L}}^{J}_{\mathcal{N}}
is equipped with a projection map from
\mathbb{L}
, the weight spaces
V^{\mathcal{N}}_{n,m}
can be specialised to weight spaces over this quotient ring. Let us denote them by
V^{\mathcal{N}}_{n,m}\mid_{\hat{\mathbb{L}}^{J}_{\mathcal{N}}}
.
There is an induced
\beta_{n}
action on these, which we denote by
\varphi^{\mathcal{N}}(\beta_{n})
. In the next part we denote by
qtr_{2,..,n}
the partial quantum trace of a braid action (we refer to
[
29
]
for the precise formula).

Our invariant
{\large\hat{{\Huge{\Gamma}}}^{J}}(K)
for a knot
K=\hat{\beta_{n}}
corresponds to a sum of quantum partial traces of the induced
\beta_{n}
actions
\varphi^{\mathcal{N},m}(\beta_{n})
on all the weight spaces over the quotient ring
\hat{\mathbb{L}}^{J}_{\mathcal{N}}
:

\{V^{\mathcal{N}}_{n,m}\mid_{\hat{\mathbb{L}}^{J}_{\mathcal{N}}}\mid 0\leqslant m\leqslant(n-1)(\mathcal{N}-1)\}.

More precisely, we have:

(10.10)

\hat{\Gamma}^{\mathcal{N},J}(K)=\sum_{m=0}^{(n-1)(\mathcal{N}-1)}qtr_{2,..,n}\left(\varphi^{\mathcal{N},m}(\beta_{n})\right)(v_{0})

We have as well a well-defined induced action on the weight spaces
\hat{V}_{n,m}
. We denote it by
\hat{\varphi}^{m}(\beta_{n})
.

Lemma 10.11

If we fix
\mathcal{N}\in\mathbb{N}
, then for any
m\in\mathbb{N}
, the partial trace of the generic braid action
\hat{\varphi}^{m}(\beta_{n})
specialised over the quotient ring
\hat{\mathbb{L}}^{J}_{\mathcal{N}}
, becomes equal to the partial trace of the action on the level
\mathcal{N}
weight space:

(10.11)

qtr_{2,..,n}\left(\varphi^{\mathcal{N},m}(\beta_{n})\right)(v_{0})=\left(qtr_{2,..,n}\hat{\varphi}^{m}(\beta_{n})\right)(v_{0})\mid_{\hat{\mathbb{L}}^{J}_{\mathcal{N}}},\forall m\in\mathbb{N}.

The proof of this fact follows in an analog manner as the proof of Lemma
7.3
. The main idea is that if we have the partial trace with respect to
v_{0}
, and we work with braids that give knots by braid closure, then the extra vectors from
\hat{V}_{n,m}
which do not belong to
V^{\mathcal{N}}_{n,m}
get coefficients that belong to the ideal
\tilde{I}^{J}_{\mathcal{N}}
, and so they get traced by zero when looking in the quotient ring
\hat{\mathbb{L}}^{J}_{\mathcal{N}}
.

Folowing
[
31
]
, the Habiro-Willetts invariant is the sum of traces of generic representations on weight spaces on the Verma module:

(10.12)

{\large\hat{{\Huge{\Gamma}}}^{W}}(K)=\sum_{m}qtr_{2,..,n}\left(\hat{\varphi}^{m}(\beta_{n})\right)(v_{0})

Let us denote by
{\large\hat{{\Huge{\Gamma}}}^{W}}(K)_{\mathcal{N}}
the
\mathcal{N}^{th}
component of
{\large\hat{{\Huge{\Gamma}}}^{W}}(K)
in
\hat{\mathbb{L}}^{W}_{\mathcal{N}}
.
Then,
{\large\hat{{\Huge{\Gamma}}}^{W}}(K)_{\mathcal{N}}
is obtained by considering the image
{\large\hat{{\Huge{\Gamma}}}^{W}}(K)
in the corresponding quotient ring:

{\large\hat{{\Huge{\Gamma}}}^{W}}(K)_{\mathcal{N}}={\large\hat{{\Huge{\Gamma}}}^{W}}(K)\mid_{\hat{\mathbb{L}}^{W}_{\mathcal{N}}}=\sum_{m}qtr_{2,..,n}\left(\hat{\varphi}^{m}(\beta_{n})\right)(v_{0})\mid_{\hat{\mathbb{L}}^{W}_{\mathcal{N}}}.

Since
\tilde{I}^{J}_{\mathcal{N}}\subseteq\tilde{I}^{W}_{\mathcal{N}}
and using formulas (
10.10
) and (
10.11
) for our invariant at level
\mathcal{N}
we have:

(10.13)

\displaystyle{\large\hat{{\Huge{\Gamma}}}^{W}}(K)_{\mathcal{N}}

\displaystyle={\large\hat{{\Huge{\Gamma}}}^{W}}(K)\mid_{\hat{\mathbb{L}}^{W}_{\mathcal{N}}}=

\displaystyle=\sum_{m}qtr_{2,..,n}\left(\hat{\varphi}^{m}(\beta_{n})\right)(v_{0})\mid_{\hat{\mathbb{L}}^{W}_{\mathcal{N}}}=

\displaystyle=\pi_{\mathcal{N}}\left(\sum_{m}qtr_{2,..,n}\left(\hat{\varphi}^{m}(\beta_{n})\right)(v_{0})\mid_{\hat{\mathbb{L}}^{J}_{\mathcal{N}}}\right)=

\displaystyle=\pi_{\mathcal{N}}\left(\sum_{m}qtr_{2,..,n}\left(\varphi^{\mathcal{N},m}(\beta_{n})\right)(v_{0})\right)=

\displaystyle=\pi_{\mathcal{N}}\left(\hat{\Gamma}^{\mathcal{N},J}(K)\right).

This means that
\pi_{\mathcal{N}}\left(\hat{\Gamma}^{\mathcal{N},J}(K)\right)={\large\hat{{\Huge{\Gamma}}}^{W}}(K)_{\mathcal{N}}
for all levels
\mathcal{N}
, so our universal geometric Jones universal invariant recovers Habiro-Willetts’s universal invariant, if we project onto the ring
\hat{\mathbb{L}}^{W}
:

\pi:\hat{\mathbb{L}}^{J}\xrightarrow{\ }\mathrel{\mkern-14.0mu}\rightarrow\hat{\mathbb{L}}^{W}

(10.14)

\pi({\large\hat{{\Huge{\Gamma}}}^{J}}(K))={\large\hat{{\Huge{\Gamma}}}^{W}}(K).

which concludes Theorem
10.5
.
∎

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbb{L}}^{J}=\underset{\longleftarrow}{\mathrm{lim}}\ \hat{\mathbb{L}}^{J}_{\mathcal{N}}

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbb{L}}^{J}_{\mathcal{N}}=\mathbb{Z}[x^{\pm 1},d^{\pm 1}]/\langle\prod_{i=1}^{\mathcal{N}}(xd^{i-1}-1)\rangle.

\hat{\mathbb{L}}^{J}_{\mathcal{N}}\ni\hat{\Gamma}^{\mathcal{N},J}(K)
\hat{\mathbb{L}}^{W}_{\mathcal{N}}
J_{\mathcal{N}}(K)
J_{\mathcal{N}-1}(K)
J_{\mathcal{M}}(K)
\frac{\Phi^{\mathcal{N}}(K)}{\Delta(x^{2\mathcal{N}})}
\frac{\Phi^{\mathcal{N}-1}(K)}{\Delta(x^{2(\mathcal{N}-1})}
\frac{\Phi^{\mathcal{M}}(K)}{\Delta(x^{2\mathcal{M}})}
\mathcal{N}^{th}
unified Jones inv.
Universal Coloured Jones inv.
Habiro-Willetts universal invariant
{\large\hat{{\Huge{\Gamma}}}^{J}}(K)\in\hat{\mathbb{L}}^{J}
{\large\hat{{\Huge{\Gamma}}}^{W}}(K)\in\hat{\mathbb{L}}^{W}
\nexists
\pi
\underset{\longleftarrow}{\mathrm{lim}}

(10.15)

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbb{L}}^{W}=\underset{\longleftarrow}{\mathrm{lim}}\ \hat{\mathbb{L}}^{W}_{\mathcal{N}}

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbb{L}}^{W}_{\mathcal{N}}=\mathbb{Z}[x^{\pm 1},d^{\pm 1}]/\langle\prod_{i=1}^{k-1}(xd^{i-1}-1)\prod_{i=k-1}^{\mathcal{N}-1}(d^{i}-1)\mid 1\leqslant k\leqslant\mathcal{N}-1\rangle.
